```
usage: desloppify plan [-h] [--state STATE] [--output FILE]
                       {show,queue,reset,move,describe,note,skip,unskip,reopen,done,focus,cluster,synthesize}
                       ...

Manage the living plan — a persistent layer on top of the work queue.
Track custom ordering, clusters, skips, and per-finding annotations.
Run with no subcommand to generate a full prioritized markdown plan.

positional arguments:
  {show,queue,reset,move,describe,note,skip,unskip,reopen,done,focus,cluster,synthesize}
    show                Show plan metadata summary
    queue               Compact table of upcoming queue items
    reset               Reset plan to empty
    move                Move findings in the queue
    describe            Set augmented description
    note                Set note on findings
    skip                Skip findings: temporary (default), --permanent
                        (wontfix), or --false-positive
    unskip              Bring skipped findings back to queue (reopens
                        permanent/fp in state)
    reopen              Reopen resolved findings and move back to queue
    done                Mark findings as fixed (shows score movement + next
                        step)
    focus               Set or clear active cluster focus
    cluster             Manage finding clusters
    synthesize          Staged synthesis workflow for review findings

options:
  -h, --help            show this help message and exit
  --state STATE         Path to state file
  --output FILE         Write to file instead of stdout

typical workflow:
  desloppify scan                       # detect findings
  desloppify plan                       # full prioritized markdown
  desloppify plan queue                 # compact table of all items
  desloppify plan cluster create ...    # group related findings
  desloppify plan focus <cluster>       # narrow scope
  desloppify next                       # work on the next item
  desloppify plan done <id> --attest .. # mark as fixed

subcommands:
  show       Show plan metadata summary
  queue      Compact table of upcoming queue items
  reset      Reset plan to empty
  move       Move findings in the queue
  done       Mark findings as fixed (score movement + next-step)
  describe   Set augmented description
  note       Set note on findings
  skip       Skip findings (temporary/permanent/false_positive)
  unskip     Bring skipped findings back to queue
  reopen     Reopen resolved findings
  focus      Set or clear active cluster focus
  cluster    Manage finding clusters
```

Excellent. Now I have a thorough understanding of all 46 findings. Let me analyze this carefully before writing the observe report.

## My Analysis of Themes, Root Causes, and Contradictions

**Theme 1: Batch/Review Orchestration Fragmentation** (largest cluster, ~10-12 findings)
Findings f41b3eb7, 74ed4443, 447178dd, e5a7a88f, f26ffe39, eba5d6b3, 22f7ba79, 15d0e0a8, e54cd96b, 9feb4a18 all describe the same fundamental problem: the review/batch subsystem is over-decomposed with too many wrapper layers, callback bags, and duplicated flow control. These are highly convergent -- no contradictions, just the same root cause observed through different dimensional lenses.

**Theme 2: Facade/API Boundary Confusion** (~8 findings)
Findings 7fd735cf, dbf9f510, 33e86a45, f7be360f, c8ad41ba, 80186ac4, eb04e7f4, cf62ed92 all describe overlapping core facades and unclear ownership. state.py as a barrel export, multiple path/text APIs with divergent semantics, dynamic imports as structural glue. Again convergent.

**Theme 3: Registry/Init Coupling** (3 near-identical findings)
Findings 44e4498f, 1b5606e5, b9a5793d are essentially the same finding expressed three times: language registry uses mutable process-global state. All suggest the same fix: explicit LanguageRegistryContext object.

**Theme 4: CLI Command Duplication** (~6 findings)
Findings 45d54f92, 0e43a64d, 35546417, 8760788f, d7ed5914, 0b3fbc6c describe duplicated patterns across CLI commands -- warning preflight, score formatting, detector orchestration, formatting branches.

**Theme 5: Contract/Type Tightening** (~5 findings)
Findings d622e00c, a037b922, 4e644e11, a3ea4c61, 0b3fbc6c describe weak typing: .get() fallback on TypedDicts, untyped dict payloads, loose scope fields, bypassed runtime contracts.

**Theme 6: Plan/Cluster Handler Decomposition** (2-3 findings)
Findings 74c70d58, 28dba24f, 00b66037 describe command handlers mixing concerns.

**Theme 7: Standalone/Misc** (remaining)
b476708f (TS smell detector mixing domains), 8780e2a7 (dead private helper), 1137af2b (incomplete tuple API migration), 33465ef2 (scan-root resolution), d1211dbc (import cycle), 8784ea85 (error masking in move), 74c47e63 (C# error dropping), 41ebdc28 (untested coloring), 0604dbf5 (untested replay import).

**Potential Contradictions to Flag:**
1. The facade findings suggest both "consolidate into fewer modules" (7fd735cf, dbf9f510) AND "split into more focused modules" (f7be360f, c8ad41ba, 80186ac4). These are not truly contradictory -- the resolution is: consolidate overlapping facades into ONE canonical module per concern, then split the current mega-modules along concern boundaries. But the apparent tension needs acknowledgment.
2. The batch orchestration findings suggest both "collapse wrappers into one service" AND "explicit subcomponents." Again not contradictory -- one service with internal structure -- but needs clear direction.

    44→
    45→def _short_id(fid: str) -> str:
    46→    """Extract the 8-char hash suffix from a finding ID for compact display.
    47→
    48→    Finding IDs look like ``review::.::holistic::dim::identifier::abcdef12``.
    49→    Commands accept the hash suffix as a shorthand for the full ID.
    50→    """
    51→    if "::" in fid:
    52→        suffix = fid.rsplit("::", 1)[-1]
    53→        if len(suffix) >= 8:
    54→            return suffix[:8]
    55→    return fid
    56→
    57→
    58→def _synthesis_coverage(plan: dict) -> tuple[int, int, dict]:
    59→    """Return (organized, total, clusters) for synthesis progress."""
    60→    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
    61→    all_cluster_ids: set[str] = set()
    62→    for c in clusters.values():
    63→        all_cluster_ids.update(c.get("finding_ids", []))
    64→    queue_ids = [fid for fid in plan.get("queue_order", []) if fid != SYNTHESIS_ID]
    65→    organized = sum(1 for fid in queue_ids if fid in all_cluster_ids)
    66→    return organized, len(queue_ids), clusters
    67→
    68→
    69→def _unenriched_clusters(plan: dict) -> list[tuple[str, list[str]]]:
    70→    """Return clusters with findings that are missing required enrichment.
    71→
    72→    Returns list of (cluster_name, list_of_missing_fields).
    73→    Only checks non-auto clusters (manual clusters the agent creates).
    74→    """
    75→    gaps: list[tuple[str, list[str]]] = []
    76→    for name, cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).items():
    77→        if not cluster.get("finding_ids"):
    78→            continue
    79→        if cluster.get("auto"):
    80→            continue
    81→        missing: list[str] = []
    82→        if not cluster.get("description"):
    83→            missing.append("description")
    84→        if not cluster.get("action_steps"):
    85→            missing.append("action_steps")
    86→        if missing:
    87→            gaps.append((name, missing))
    88→    return gaps
    89→
    90→
    91→def _manual_clusters_with_findings(plan: dict) -> list[str]:
    92→    """Return names of non-auto clusters that have findings."""
    93→    return [
    94→        name for name, c in plan.get("clusters", {}).items()
    95→        if c.get("finding_ids") and not c.get("auto")
    96→    ]

    error-and-correctness: 10 items [desc, 9 steps] — Fix error handling gaps and logic correctness issues: stop masking import failures as unsupported-language errors, surface Roslyn failure reasons, enforce explicit scan-root semantics, gate partial-failure imports correctly, add missing test coverage for coloring and replay import paths, and decompose mixed-concern command handlers.
    batch-orchestration: 9 items [desc, 3 steps] — Consolidate review/batch orchestration from fragmented wrappers and callback bags into a cohesive service with bounded collaborator interfaces. This is the single highest-leverage structural improvement.
    facade-boundaries: 8 items [desc, 3 steps] — Establish canonical import boundaries: one module per concern, consolidate overlapping core facades, split state.py into focused modules, and remove pass-through barrel exports.

---

## Retrospective

### 1. What was easy/intuitive?

- The **stage progression** (observe -> reflect -> organize -> complete) was logical and easy to follow. Each stage had clear instructions about what to do next printed in the output.
- The **dashboard** was excellent -- it showed all 46 findings grouped by dimension with truncated action items, making it easy to analyze themes without needing separate commands.
- The **finding hash shorthand** (e.g., `f41b3eb7`) made it easy to reference specific findings in reports without typing full IDs.
- The overall concept of the synthesis workflow -- analyze, strategize, organize, confirm -- is a sensible approach to triaging a batch of review findings.

### 2. What was confusing or required multiple attempts?

- **The synthesis needed to be started explicitly** with `--start`. The initial dashboard showed the synthesis stages and even said "current" next to the observe stage, but running `--stage observe` failed with "synthesis::pending is not in the queue". The error message was clear but the need for `--start` was not obvious from the dashboard.
- **Stale `.pyc` files** caused `ImportError: cannot import name 'check_synthesis_guardrail'` that didn't match the actual file contents. This was a development environment issue (partially applied changes), not a synthesis workflow issue, but it was very confusing. The error pointed to code that didn't exist on disk.
- **The attestation requirements** required trial and error. First attempt failed because I didn't mention specific dimension names. The error message was helpful ("Attestation must reference at least one dimension from the summary") but it would be even better if the initial prompt explained the requirements upfront.
- **`plan skip` with short hash** didn't work for finding `59fcc618` -- I had to use the full ID. But `plan skip b476708f` worked fine. Inconsistent behavior.

### 3. What error messages were helpful vs unhelpful?

**Helpful:**
- "synthesis::pending is not in the queue -- nothing to observe" -- clear about the prerequisite.
- "Attestation must reference at least one dimension from the summary. Mention one of: abstraction_fitness, design_coherence..." -- told me exactly what was needed and even listed valid options.
- "Now confirm your analysis." with the exact command to run -- good progressive disclosure.
- "Cannot reflect: observe stage not confirmed" -- clear gating with the fix command.

**Unhelpful:**
- "No matching findings found" when `plan skip 59fcc618` failed -- didn't explain why or suggest using the full ID. Could have said "No queue items match this pattern. Try the full finding ID."

### 4. What would you change about the workflow?

- **Combine confirm into stage completion**: The observe -> confirm observe -> reflect -> confirm reflect -> organize -> confirm organize -> complete flow is 7 steps for what could be 4 (observe -> reflect -> organize -> complete). The confirmations add friction without adding much value when an agent is driving the workflow. Consider making confirmation optional or combining it with the next stage submission.
- **Auto-start synthesis**: If the dashboard detects `synthesis::pending` in the queue, the observe command should auto-start rather than requiring a separate `--start` flag.
- **Show attestation requirements upfront**: When printing "confirm with --attestation", also print what the attestation must contain (e.g., "must reference at least one dimension name, minimum 30 chars").
- **More concise dashboard**: The full finding list is printed on every `plan synthesize` invocation. After the observe stage, I already know the findings -- subsequent stages could show a more compact summary unless `--verbose` is passed.
- **Consistent short-hash support**: Either always support 8-char hash shorthand for finding operations, or never. The inconsistency between `plan skip` and `show` was confusing.

{
  "command": "python -c \"\nimport json\nwith open('.desloppify/state-python.json') as f:\n    state = json.load(f)\n\n# Get all current open review finding IDs\ncurrent_review_ids = set()\nfor key, val in state.get('findings', {}).items():\n    if isinstance(val, dict) and val.get('detector') == 'review' and val.get('status') == 'open':\n        current_review_ids.add(key)\n\n# Get hash suffixes of current findings\ncurrent_hashes = set()\nfor fid in current_review_ids:\n    if '::' in fid:\n        h = fid.rsplit('::', 1)[-1][:8]\n        current_hashes.add(h)\n\nprint(f'Current open review finding hashes ({len(current_hashes)}):')\nfor h in sorted(current_hashes):\n    print(f'  {h}')\n\n# Check which cluster finding IDs are NOT in current findings\nwith open('.desloppify/plan.json') as f:\n    plan = json.load(f)\n\nfor name, cluster in sorted(plan.get('clusters', {}).items()):\n    fids = cluster.get('finding_ids', [])\n    if not fids or cluster.get('auto'):\n        continue\n    stale = []\n    for fid in fids:\n        h = fid.rsplit('::', 1)[-1][:8] if '::' in fid else fid[:8]\n        if h not in current_hashes:\n            stale.append(h)\n    if stale:\n        print(f'\\n{name}: {len(stale)}/{len(fids)} findings are stale/disappeared')\n        for h in stale:\n            print(f'  STALE: {h}')\n\" 2>&1\n",
  "description": "Check which cluster finding IDs are current vs stale"
}

---

## Detailed Retrospective: The Synthesis Workflow

### 1. What was easy/intuitive?

**The staged workflow structure (observe -> reflect -> organize -> complete)** was well-designed and logically sound. Each stage builds on the previous one, and the tool enforces sequential progression. The idea of analyzing first, forming strategy second, then organizing, and finally confirming is a natural cognitive flow for this kind of work.

**The synthesis dashboard** was excellent. Showing all 46 findings organized by dimension with their suggestions gave me a complete picture in one view. The prompt to "look for contradictions: findings in the same dimension that recommend opposite changes" was the right guidance at the right time.

**The cluster management commands** (create, add, update, delete, show, list) were straightforward and predictable. The `cluster update --steps` command for adding action steps was intuitive.

**The final completion flow** was clean -- providing a strategy summary and having the tool print a nice summary of what was organized was satisfying.

### 2. What was confusing or required multiple attempts?

**The initial setup was the biggest confusion.** The task told me `synthesis::pending` was at the top of the work queue, but it was not actually there. The `synthesis::pending` item is only auto-injected by `sync_synthesis_needed()` when the review finding hash changes, and the hashes matched because a previous synthesis had already completed. I had to manually inject it into the queue by editing plan.json directly. This is a significant onboarding/setup issue -- the synthesis workflow has no obvious "start synthesis" command; it relies on automatic injection during scan reconciliation.

**The `plan cluster add` command initially failed** when I tried using 8-character hash suffixes (e.g., `f41b3eb7`). It returned "No matching findings found" silently. I had to extract full finding IDs from the state file (which are long strings like `review::.::holistic::abstraction_fitness::review_runner_param_bag_churn::f41b3eb7`) and use those instead. This was a significant friction point because the dashboard explicitly shows short hashes and says "Use hash in commands: `desloppify plan skip <hash>`" -- but `cluster add` doesn't accept them.

**The noise budget in `show review --status open`** was frustrating. It limited output to 10 findings even with `--top 100`, hiding 36 of the 46 findings. The synthesis dashboard showed all 46, but if I wanted to examine individual findings in detail, I had to read the state JSON directly. There should be a way to bypass the noise budget for exploratory work.

**Understanding the existing cluster state was tedious.** Many clusters from previous synthesis rounds referenced finding IDs that no longer existed in the current state. I had to write Python scripts to cross-reference cluster finding IDs against current open findings to discover that 3 clusters were entirely stale and 2 clusters were exact duplicates.

### 3. What error messages were helpful vs unhelpful?

**Helpful:**
- `"synthesis::pending is not in the queue -- nothing to observe."` -- This was clear and diagnostic when I first tried to run the observe stage before injecting the synthesis ID. It told me exactly what was wrong.
- `"Observe stage recorded: 46 findings analysed."` -- Clear success feedback with relevant count.
- `"Organize stage recorded: 6 enriched cluster(s)."` followed by a detailed listing of each cluster with finding count and step count -- very useful for verification.
- `"Added 11 item(s) to cluster review-subsystem."` -- Clear confirmation.

**Unhelpful:**
- `"No matching findings found."` when using short hashes with `cluster add` -- this gave no hint that full IDs are required. It should say something like "Could not match pattern 'f41b3eb7'. Finding IDs must match state entries. Try the full ID from `desloppify show`."
- The noise budget silently hiding 36 findings with only `"Noise budget: 10/detector (36 hidden: review: +36)"` -- this is informational but provides no way to override it. A `--no-budget` flag would help.

### 4. Where did you feel the tool was fighting you vs helping you?

**Fighting me:**
- **No "start synthesis" command.** The synthesis workflow depends on `synthesis::pending` being auto-injected by scan reconciliation, but there's no explicit way to trigger it manually. I had to edit plan.json directly to start the workflow.
- **Short hashes not accepted by `cluster add`.** The dashboard encourages using short hashes ("Use hash in commands: `desloppify plan skip <hash>`") but the `cluster add` command does not resolve them. This created a jarring inconsistency where I had to switch from the tool's UI to raw JSON manipulation.
- **No way to merge clusters.** When I identified that `batch-orchestration` and `review-consolidation` should be one cluster, I had to create a new cluster, manually add all findings, then delete the old ones. A `cluster merge <source> <target>` command would save significant effort.
- **Stale cluster references.** Clusters referencing disappeared findings are not cleaned up automatically. Having 3 clusters with 20 stale finding IDs created confusion and required manual investigation.
- **The `--report` parameter being on the command line** means long reports require careful shell escaping. A `--report-file` option or interactive stdin input would be more ergonomic for multi-paragraph analysis.

**Helping me:**
- **The synthesis dashboard** is genuinely excellent. It shows exactly what stage you're at, what the next command is, and provides all the data you need (findings by dimension, current clusters, stage progress indicator).
- **The stage gates** with minimum character requirements (100 chars for observe, 200 for strategy) prevent rushing through without genuine analysis.
- **The "Strategic briefing" box** after the reflect stage clearly presents the strategy for user review -- good UX for a human-in-the-loop workflow.
- **The cluster show command** with its nice formatted output including all members and their summaries was very useful for verification.

### 5. What would you change about the workflow?

1. **Add a `plan synthesize --start` command** that explicitly injects `synthesis::pending` into the queue and resets synthesis stages, rather than relying on automatic injection via scan reconciliation.

2. **Make short hashes work everywhere.** If `plan skip <hash>` works with 8-char hashes, then `cluster add <name> <hash>` should too. This is the single biggest friction point.

3. **Add a `cluster merge <source> <target>` command** that moves all findings from source to target and deletes source. The common case during synthesis is consolidating overlapping clusters.

4. **Auto-clean stale cluster references** during synthesis startup. If a cluster's finding IDs are 100% stale, either delete it automatically or flag it clearly in the dashboard with "[STALE]".

5. **Add a `--no-budget` or `--all` flag to `show`** to bypass the noise budget when doing exploratory analysis. During synthesis, you need to see all findings, not a capped subset.

6. **Support `--report-file <path>` as an alternative to `--report "..."`** for stage reports. Shell escaping of multi-paragraph text with special characters is fragile.

7. **Show finding membership in the dashboard.** When listing findings by dimension, indicate which cluster each finding is in (or mark as unclustered). Currently you have to cross-reference mentally.

8. **Clean up empty clusters from previous rounds.** The plan currently shows 35 clusters, of which 14 are empty. This noise makes it harder to see the 6 that matter. A `plan cluster prune-empty` command or automatic hiding of empty clusters would help.

1→"""State filtering, ignore rules, and finding pattern matching."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import fnmatch
     6→import re
     7→
     8→__all__ = [
     9→    "finding_in_scan_scope",
    10→    "open_scope_breakdown",
    11→    "path_scoped_findings",
    12→    "is_ignored",
    13→    "matched_ignore_pattern",
    14→    "remove_ignored_findings",
    15→    "add_ignore",
    16→    "make_finding",
    17→]
    18→
    19→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import (
    20→    Finding,
    21→    StateModel,
    22→    ensure_state_defaults,
    23→    utc_now,
    24→    validate_state_invariants,
    25→)
    26→from desloppify.core.discovery_api import rel
    27→
    28→
    29→def path_scoped_findings(
    30→    findings: dict[str, Finding],
    31→    scan_path: str | None,
    32→) -> dict[str, Finding]:
    33→    """Filter findings to those within the given scan path."""
    34→    return {
    35→        finding_id: finding
    36→        for finding_id, finding in findings.items()
    37→        if finding_in_scan_scope(str(finding.get("file", "")), scan_path)
    38→    }
    39→
    40→
    41→def finding_in_scan_scope(file_path: str, scan_path: str | None) -> bool:
    42→    """Return True when a file path belongs to the active scan scope."""
    43→    if not scan_path or scan_path == ".":
    44→        return True
    45→    prefix = scan_path.rstrip("/") + "/"
    46→    return (
    47→        file_path.startswith(prefix)
    48→        or file_path == scan_path
    49→        or file_path == "."
    50→    )
    51→
    52→
    53→def open_scope_breakdown(
    54→    findings: dict[str, Finding],
    55→    scan_path: str | None,
    56→    *,
    57→    detector: str | None = None,
    58→) -> dict[str, int]:
    59→    """Return open-finding counts split by in-scope vs out-of-scope carryover."""
    60→    in_scope = 0
    61→    out_of_scope = 0
    62→
    63→    for finding in findings.values():
    64→        if finding.get("suppressed"):
    65→            continue
    66→        if finding.get("status") != "open":
    67→            continue
    68→        if detector is not None and finding.get("detector") != detector:
    69→            continue
    70→        file_path = str(finding.get("file", ""))
    71→        if finding_in_scan_scope(file_path, scan_path):
    72→            in_scope += 1
    73→        else:
    74→            out_of_scope += 1
    75→
    76→    return {
    77→        "in_scope": in_scope,
    78→        "out_of_scope": out_of_scope,
    79→        "global": in_scope + out_of_scope,
    80→    }
    81→
    82→
    83→def is_ignored(finding_id: str, file: str, ignore_patterns: list[str]) -> bool:
    84→    """Check if a finding matches any ignore pattern (glob, ID prefix, or file path)."""
    85→    return matched_ignore_pattern(finding_id, file, ignore_patterns) is not None
    86→
    87→
    88→def matched_ignore_pattern(
    89→    finding_id: str, file: str, ignore_patterns: list[str]
    90→) -> str | None:
    91→    """Return the ignore pattern that matched, if any."""
    92→    for pattern in ignore_patterns:
    93→        if "*" in pattern:
    94→            target = finding_id if "::" in pattern else file
    95→            if fnmatch.fnmatch(target, pattern):
    96→                return pattern
    97→            continue
    98→
    99→        if "::" in pattern:
   100→            if finding_id.startswith(pattern):
   101→                return pattern
   102→            continue
   103→
   104→        if file == pattern or file == rel(pattern):
   105→            return pattern
   106→
   107→    return None
   108→
   109→
   110→def remove_ignored_findings(state: StateModel, pattern: str) -> int:
   111→    """Suppress findings matching an ignore pattern. Returns count affected."""
   112→    ensure_state_defaults(state)
   113→    matched_ids = [
   114→        finding_id
   115→        for finding_id, finding in state["findings"].items()
   116→        if is_ignored(finding_id, finding["file"], [pattern])
   117→    ]
   118→    now = utc_now()
   119→    for finding_id in matched_ids:
   120→        finding = state["findings"][finding_id]
   121→        finding["suppressed"] = True
   122→        finding["suppressed_at"] = now
   123→        finding["suppression_pattern"] = pattern
   124→    # Deferred import to avoid circular dependency with engine._state.scoring
   125→    from desloppify.engine._state.scoring import _recompute_stats
   126→
   127→    _recompute_stats(state, scan_path=state.get("scan_path"))
   128→    validate_state_invariants(state)
   129→    return len(matched_ids)
   130→
   131→
   132→def add_ignore(state: StateModel, pattern: str) -> int:
   133→    """Add an ignore pattern and remove existing matching findings."""
   134→    ensure_state_defaults(state)
   135→    config = state.setdefault("config", {})
   136→    ignores = config.setdefault("ignore", [])
   137→    if pattern not in ignores:
   138→        ignores.append(pattern)
   139→    return remove_ignored_findings(state, pattern)
   140→
   141→
   142→def make_finding(
   143→    detector: str,
   144→    file: str,
   145→    name: str,
   146→    *,
   147→    tier: int,
   148→    confidence: str,
   149→    summary: str,
   150→    detail: dict | None = None,
   151→) -> Finding:
   152→    """Create a normalized finding dict with a stable ID."""
   153→    rfile = rel(file)
   154→    finding_id = f"{detector}::{rfile}::{name}" if name else f"{detector}::{rfile}"
   155→    now = utc_now()
   156→    return {
   157→        "id": finding_id,
   158→        "detector": detector,
   159→        "file": rfile,
   160→        "tier": tier,
   161→        "confidence": confidence,
   162→        "summary": summary,
   163→        "detail": detail or {},
   164→        "status": "open",
   165→        "note": None,
   166→        "first_seen": now,
   167→        "last_seen": now,
   168→        "resolved_at": None,
   169→        "reopen_count": 0,
   170→    }
   171→
   172→
   173→_HEX8_RE = re.compile(r'^[0-9a-f]{8}$')
   174→
   175→
   176→def _matches_pattern(finding_id: str, finding: dict[str, str], pattern: str) -> bool:
   177→    """Check if a finding matches by ID, glob, prefix, detector, suffix, or path."""
   178→    if finding_id == pattern:
   179→        return True
   180→    if "*" in pattern and fnmatch.fnmatch(finding_id, pattern):
   181→        return True
   182→    if "::" in pattern and finding_id.startswith(pattern):
   183→        return True
   184→    if _HEX8_RE.match(pattern) and finding_id.endswith("::" + pattern):
   185→        return True
   186→    return (
   187→        finding.get("detector") == pattern
   188→        or finding["file"] == pattern
   189→        or finding["file"].startswith(pattern.rstrip("/") + "/")
   190→    )
   191→

1→"""Pattern → finding-ID resolution for plan commands."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import PlanModel
     6→from desloppify.state import StateModel, match_findings
     7→
     8→
     9→def resolve_ids_from_patterns(
    10→    state: StateModel,
    11→    patterns: list[str],
    12→    *,
    13→    plan: PlanModel | None = None,
    14→    status_filter: str = "open",
    15→) -> list[str]:
    16→    """Resolve one or more patterns to a deduplicated list of finding IDs.
    17→
    18→    When *plan* is provided, literal IDs that exist only in the plan
    19→    (e.g. ``subjective::*`` synthetic items) are included even if they
    20→    have no corresponding entry in ``state["findings"]``.
    21→    """
    22→    seen: set[str] = set()
    23→    result: list[str] = []
    24→
    25→    # Collect all plan IDs for literal-match fallback
    26→    plan_ids: set[str] = set()
    27→    if plan is not None:
    28→        plan_ids.update(plan.get("queue_order", []))
    29→        plan_ids.update(plan.get("skipped", {}).keys())
    30→        for cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).values():
    31→            plan_ids.update(cluster.get("finding_ids", []))
    32→
    33→    for pattern in patterns:
    34→        matches = match_findings(state, pattern, status_filter=status_filter)
    35→        if matches:
    36→            for finding in matches:
    37→                fid = finding["id"]
    38→                if fid not in seen:
    39→                    seen.add(fid)
    40→                    result.append(fid)
    41→        elif pattern in plan_ids and pattern not in seen:
    42→            # Literal plan ID (e.g. subjective::foo) not in state findings
    43→            seen.add(pattern)
    44→            result.append(pattern)
    45→    return result
    46→
    47→
    48→__all__ = ["resolve_ids_from_patterns"]
    49→
```

1→"""State resolution operations (match + resolve)."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import copy
     6→
     7→__all__ = [
     8→    "coerce_assessment_score",
     9→    "match_findings",
    10→    "resolve_findings",
    11→]
    12→
    13→from desloppify.core._internal.text_utils import is_numeric
    14→from desloppify.engine._state.filtering import _matches_pattern
    15→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import (
    16→    StateModel,
    17→    ensure_state_defaults,
    18→    utc_now,
    19→    validate_state_invariants,
    20→)
    21→from desloppify.engine._state.scoring import _recompute_stats
    22→
    23→
    24→def coerce_assessment_score(value: object) -> float | None:
    25→    """Normalize a subjective assessment score payload to a 0-100 float.
    26→
    27→    Returns ``None`` when the value cannot be interpreted as a numeric score
    28→    (e.g. bools, non-numeric strings, missing keys).
    29→    """
    30→    if is_numeric(value):
    31→        return round(max(0.0, min(100.0, float(value))), 1)
    32→    if isinstance(value, dict):
    33→        raw = value.get("score")
    34→        if not is_numeric(raw):
    35→            return None
    36→        return round(max(0.0, min(100.0, float(raw))), 1)
    37→    return None
    38→
    39→
    40→def _mark_stale_assessments_on_review_resolve(
    41→    state: StateModel,
    42→    *,
    43→    status: str,
    44→    resolved_findings: list[dict],
    45→    now: str,
    46→) -> None:
    47→    """Mark subjective assessments as stale when review findings are resolved.
    48→
    49→    The assessment score is preserved (not zeroed) — only a fresh review import
    50→    should change dimension scores.  The stale marker tells the UI to prompt
    51→    for a re-review.
    52→    """
    53→    assessments = state.get("subjective_assessments")
    54→    if not isinstance(assessments, dict) or not assessments:
    55→        return
    56→
    57→    touched_dimensions: set[str] = set()
    58→    for finding in resolved_findings:
    59→        if finding.get("detector") != "review":
    60→            continue
    61→        dimension = str(finding.get("detail", {}).get("dimension", "")).strip()
    62→        if dimension:
    63→            touched_dimensions.add(dimension)
    64→
    65→    for dimension in sorted(touched_dimensions):
    66→        if dimension not in assessments:
    67→            continue
    68→
    69→        payload = assessments.get(dimension)
    70→        if isinstance(payload, dict):
    71→            payload["needs_review_refresh"] = True
    72→            payload["refresh_reason"] = f"review_finding_{status}"
    73→            payload["stale_since"] = now
    74→        else:
    75→            assessments[dimension] = {
    76→                "score": coerce_assessment_score(payload) or 0.0,
    77→                "needs_review_refresh": True,
    78→                "refresh_reason": f"review_finding_{status}",
    79→                "stale_since": now,
    80→            }
    81→
    82→
    83→def match_findings(
    84→    state: StateModel, pattern: str, status_filter: str = "open"
    85→) -> list[dict]:
    86→    """Return findings matching *pattern* with the given status."""
    87→    ensure_state_defaults(state)
    88→    return [
    89→        finding
    90→        for finding_id, finding in state["findings"].items()
    91→        if not finding.get("suppressed")
    92→        if (status_filter == "all" or finding["status"] == status_filter)
    93→        and _matches_pattern(finding_id, finding, pattern)
    94→    ]
    95→
    96→
    97→def resolve_findings(
    98→    state: StateModel,
    99→    pattern: str,
   100→    status: str,
   101→    note: str | None = None,
   102→    attestation: str | None = None,
   103→) -> list[str]:
   104→    """Set finding status for matches and return affected finding IDs."""
   105→    ensure_state_defaults(state)
   106→    now = utc_now()
   107→    resolved: list[str] = []
   108→    resolved_findings: list[dict] = []
   109→    status_filter = "all" if status == "open" else "open"
   110→    for finding in match_findings(state, pattern, status_filter=status_filter):
   111→        previous_status = str(finding.get("status", "open")).strip() or "open"
   112→        if status == "open" and previous_status == "open":
   113→            continue
   114→
   115→        extra_updates: dict[str, object] = {}
   116→        if status == "wontfix":
   117→            snapshot_scan_count = int(state.get("scan_count", 0) or 0)
   118→            extra_updates["wontfix_scan_count"] = snapshot_scan_count
   119→            extra_updates["wontfix_snapshot"] = {
   120→                "captured_at": now,
   121→                "scan_count": snapshot_scan_count,
   122→                "tier": finding.get("tier"),
   123→                "confidence": finding.get("confidence"),
   124→                "detail": copy.deepcopy(finding.get("detail", {})),
   125→            }
   126→        if status == "open":
   127→            finding["reopen_count"] = int(finding.get("reopen_count", 0) or 0) + 1
   128→            finding.pop("wontfix_scan_count", None)
   129→            finding.pop("wontfix_snapshot", None)
   130→            previous_note = finding.get("note")
   131→            next_note = note if note is not None else previous_note
   132→            extra_updates["resolved_at"] = None
   133→            extra_updates["note"] = next_note
   134→            extra_updates["resolution_attestation"] = {
   135→                "kind": "manual_reopen",
   136→                "text": attestation or note,
   137→                "attested_at": now,
   138→                "scan_verified": False,
   139→                "previous_status": previous_status,
   140→            }
   141→
   142→        updates: dict[str, object] = {
   143→            "status": status,
   144→            "note": note,
   145→            "resolved_at": now,
   146→            "suppressed": False,
   147→            "suppressed_at": None,
   148→            "suppression_pattern": None,
   149→            "resolution_attestation": {
   150→                "kind": "manual",
   151→                "text": attestation,
   152→                "attested_at": now,
   153→                "scan_verified": False,
   154→            },
   155→        }
   156→        updates.update(extra_updates)
   157→        finding.update(updates)
   158→        resolved.append(finding["id"])
   159→        resolved_findings.append(finding)
   160→
   161→    _mark_stale_assessments_on_review_resolve(
   162→        state,
   163→        status=status,
   164→        resolved_findings=resolved_findings,
   165→        now=now,
   166→    )
   167→
   168→    _recompute_stats(state, scan_path=state.get("scan_path"))
   169→    validate_state_invariants(state)
   170→    return resolved
   171→
```

    45→def _plan_file_for_state(state_file: Path | None) -> Path | None:

    51→def _snapshot_file(path: Path) -> str | None:
    52→    if not path.exists():
    53→        return None
    54→    return path.read_text()

    56→
    57→def _restore_file_snapshot(path: Path, snapshot: str | None) -> None:
    58→    if snapshot is None:
    59→        try:
    60→            path.unlink()
    61→        except FileNotFoundError:
    62→            return
    63→        return
    64→    safe_write_text(path, snapshot)

    67→def _save_plan_state_transactional(
    68→    *,
    69→    plan: dict,
    70→    plan_path: Path | None,
    71→    state_data: dict,
    72→    state_path_value: Path | None,
    73→) -> None:
    74→    """Persist plan+state together; rollback both files on partial write failure."""
    75→    effective_plan_path = _resolve_plan_file(plan_path)
    76→    effective_state_path = _resolve_state_file(state_path_value)
    77→    plan_snapshot = _snapshot_file(effective_plan_path)
    78→    state_snapshot = _snapshot_file(effective_state_path)
    79→
    80→    try:
    81→        state_mod.save_state(state_data, effective_state_path)
    82→        save_plan(plan, effective_plan_path)
    83→    except Exception:
    84→        _restore_file_snapshot(effective_state_path, state_snapshot)
    85→        _restore_file_snapshot(effective_plan_path, plan_snapshot)
    86→        raise

   143→def cmd_plan_skip(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   144→    """Skip findings — unified command for temporary/permanent/false-positive."""
   145→    runtime = command_runtime(args)
   146→    state = runtime.state

   148→        return

   170→                "Permanent skip" if kind == "permanent" else "False positive",

   173→            )
   174→            return

   176→            print(

   178→                file=sys.stderr,
   179→            )
   180→            return
   181→
   182→    state_file = runtime.state_path
   183→    plan_file = _plan_file_for_state(state_file)
   184→    plan = load_plan(plan_file)
   185→    finding_ids = resolve_ids_from_patterns(state, patterns, plan=plan)

   195→        for fid in finding_ids:

   200→    scan_count = state.get("scan_count", 0)
   201→    count = skip_items(

   205→        reason=reason,
   206→        note=note,
   207→        attestation=attestation,
   208→        review_after=review_after,
   209→        scan_count=scan_count,
   210→    )
   211→
   212→    # Log the skip action
   213→    append_log_entry(
   214→        plan,
   215→        "skip",
   216→        finding_ids=finding_ids,
   217→        actor="user",
   218→        note=note,
   219→        detail={"kind": kind, "reason": reason},
   220→    )
   221→    if state_data is not None:
   222→        _save_plan_state_transactional(

   230→
   231→    label = {"temporary": "Skipped", "permanent": "Wontfixed", "false_positive": "Marked false positive"}
   232→    print(colorize(f"  {label[kind]} {count} item(s).", "green"))

   284→def cmd_plan_reopen(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   285→    """Reopen resolved findings from plan context."""
   286→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])

   311→            count += 1
   312→        if fid not in order:

   314→            order.add(fid)

   331→_CLUSTER_INDIVIDUAL_THRESHOLD = 10

   333→
   334→def _check_cluster_guard(patterns: list[str], plan: dict, state: dict) -> bool:
   335→    """Return True if blocked by cluster guard, False if OK to proceed."""
   336→    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
   337→    for pattern in patterns:
   338→        if pattern in clusters:
   339→            cluster = clusters[pattern]
   340→            ids = cluster.get("finding_ids", [])
   341→            if len(ids) == 0:
   342→                print(colorize(
   343→                    f"\n  Cluster '{pattern}' is empty — add items before marking it done.\n",
   344→                    "yellow",
   345→                ))
   346→                print(colorize(
   347→                    f"  Use: desloppify plan cluster add {pattern} <finding-id>",
   348→                    "dim",
   349→                ))
   350→                return True  # blocked
   351→            if len(ids) <= _CLUSTER_INDIVIDUAL_THRESHOLD:
   352→                _print_cluster_guard(pattern, ids, state)
   353→                return True  # blocked
   354→    return False  # OK
   355→

   357→def _print_cluster_guard(cluster_name: str, finding_ids: list[str], state: dict) -> None:
   358→    findings = state.get("findings", {})
   359→    print(colorize(
   360→        f"\n  Cluster '{cluster_name}' has {len(finding_ids)} item(s) — mark them done individually first:\n",
   361→        "yellow",
   362→    ))
   363→    for fid in finding_ids:
   364→        f = findings.get(fid, {})
   365→        summary = f.get("summary", "(no summary)")[:80]
   366→        detector = f.get("detector", "?")
   367→        print(f"    {fid}  [{detector}]  {summary}")
   368→    print(colorize(
   369→        f"\n  Use: desloppify resolve <id> --status fixed --note '...' --attest '...'",
   370→        "dim",
   371→    ))
   372→    print(colorize(
   373→        f"  Or mark each done: desloppify plan done <id> --note '...' --confirm\n",
   374→        "dim",
   375→    ))

   378→def cmd_plan_done(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   379→    """Mark findings as fixed — delegates to cmd_resolve for rich UX."""
   380→    from desloppify.app.commands.resolve.cmd import cmd_resolve
   381→
   382→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
   383→    attestation: str | None = getattr(args, "attest", None)
   384→    note: str | None = getattr(args, "note", None)
   385→
   386→    # --confirm: auto-generate attestation from --note
   387→    if getattr(args, "confirm", False):
   388→        if not note:
   389→            print(colorize("  --confirm requires --note to describe what you did.", "red"))
   390→            return
   391→        attestation = f"I have actually {note} and I am not gaming the score."
   392→        args.attest = attestation
   393→
   394→    # Validate note length
   395→    if not validate_note_length(note):
   396→        show_note_length_requirement(note)
   397→        return
   398→
   399→    # Pre-validate attestation before delegating (avoids stale hint in resolve)
   400→    if not validate_attestation(attestation):
   401→        show_attestation_requirement("Plan done", attestation, ATTEST_EXAMPLE)
   402→        return
   403→
   404→    # Cluster completion guard: block bulk-completing small clusters
   405→    try:
   406→        runtime = command_runtime(args)
   407→        state = runtime.state
   408→        plan = load_plan()
   409→        if _check_cluster_guard(patterns, plan, state):
   410→            return
   411→    except (OSError, ValueError, KeyError, TypeError):
   412→        plan = None
   413→
   414→    # Log the done action (best-effort)
   415→    try:
   416→        if plan is None:
   417→            plan = load_plan()
   418→        clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
   419→        cluster_name = None
   420→        for p in patterns:
   421→            if p in clusters:
   422→                cluster_name = p
   423→                break
   424→        append_log_entry(
   425→            plan,
   426→            "done",
   427→            finding_ids=patterns,
   428→            cluster_name=cluster_name,
   429→            actor="user",
   430→            note=note,
   431→        )
   432→        save_plan(plan)
   433→    except (OSError, ValueError, KeyError, TypeError):
   434→        pass  # best-effort logging
   435→
   436→    # Build a Namespace that cmd_resolve expects
   437→    resolve_args = argparse.Namespace(
   438→        status="fixed",
   439→        patterns=patterns,
   440→        note=note,
   441→        attest=attestation,
   442→        confirm_batch_wontfix=False,
   443→        force_resolve=bool(getattr(args, "force_resolve", False)),
   444→        state=getattr(args, "state", None),
   445→        lang=getattr(args, "lang", None),
   446→        path=getattr(args, "path", None),
   447→        exclude=getattr(args, "exclude", None),
   448→    )
   449→
   450→    cmd_resolve(resolve_args)
   451→
   452→
   453→def cmd_plan_focus(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   454→    """Set or clear the active cluster focus."""
   455→    clear_flag = getattr(args, "clear", False)
   456→    cluster_name: str | None = getattr(args, "cluster_name", None)
   457→
   458→    plan = load_plan()
   459→    if clear_flag:
   460→        prev = plan.get("active_cluster")
   461→        clear_focus(plan)
   462→        append_log_entry(
   463→            plan, "focus", actor="user",
   464→            detail={"action": "clear", "previous": prev},
   465→        )
   466→        save_plan(plan)
   467→        print(colorize("  Focus cleared.", "green"))
   468→        return
   469→
   470→    if not cluster_name:
   471→        active = plan.get("active_cluster")
   472→        if active:
   473→            print(f"  Focused on: {active}")
   474→        else:
   475→            print("  No active focus.")
   476→        return
   477→
   478→    try:
   479→        set_focus(plan, cluster_name)
   480→    except ValueError as ex:
   481→        print(colorize(f"  {ex}", "red"))
   482→        return
   483→    append_log_entry(
   484→        plan, "focus", cluster_name=cluster_name, actor="user",
   485→        detail={"action": "set"},
   486→    )
   487→    save_plan(plan)
   488→    print(colorize(f"  Focused on: {cluster_name}", "green"))

1→"""show command: dig into findings by file, directory, detector, or pattern."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import argparse
     6→
     7→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.lang import resolve_lang
     8→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.query import write_query
     9→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.runtime import command_runtime
    10→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.score import target_strict_score_from_config
    11→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import require_completed_scan
    12→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.guardrails import print_synthesis_guardrail_info
    13→from desloppify.core.exception_sets import PLAN_LOAD_EXCEPTIONS
    14→from desloppify.intelligence.narrative import NarrativeContext, compute_narrative
    15→from desloppify.core.output_api import colorize
    16→from desloppify.core.skill_docs import check_skill_version
    17→from desloppify.core.tooling import check_config_staleness, check_tool_staleness
    18→from desloppify.engine.concerns import generate_concerns
    19→from desloppify.engine.plan import load_plan
    20→
    21→from .payload import ShowPayloadMeta, build_show_payload
    22→from .render import (
    23→    render_findings,
    24→    show_agent_plan,
    25→    show_subjective_followup,
    26→    write_show_output_file,
    27→)
    28→from .scope import (
    29→    _detector_names_hint,
    30→    _lookup_dimension_score,
    31→    load_matches,
    32→    resolve_entity,
    33→    resolve_noise,
    34→    resolve_show_scope,
    35→)
    36→
    37→
    38→def _show_concerns(state: dict, lang_name: str | None) -> None:
    39→    """Render current design concerns from mechanical signals."""
    40→    concerns = generate_concerns(state, lang_name=lang_name)
    41→    if not concerns:
    42→        print(colorize("  No design concerns detected.", "green"))
    43→        return
    44→
    45→    print(colorize(f"\n  {len(concerns)} design concern(s):\n", "bold"))
    46→    dismissals = state.get("concern_dismissals", {})
    47→
    48→    for i, c in enumerate(concerns, 1):
    49→        print(colorize(f"  {i}. [{c.type}] {c.file}", "cyan"))
    50→        print(f"     {c.summary}")
    51→        for ev in c.evidence:
    52→            print(colorize(f"       - {ev}", "dim"))
    53→        print(colorize(f"     ? {c.question}", "yellow"))
    54→
    55→        # Check if previously dismissed (but resurface due to changed findings).
    56→        prev = dismissals.get(c.fingerprint)
    57→        if isinstance(prev, dict):
    58→            reasoning = prev.get("reasoning", "")
    59→            if reasoning:
    60→                print(colorize(f"     (previously dismissed: {reasoning})", "dim"))
    61→        print()
    62→
    63→
    64→def _print_dimension_score(dim_data: dict, display_name: str) -> None:
    65→    """Print the health/strict score line for a dimension if available."""
    66→    score_val = dim_data.get("score") if isinstance(dim_data, dict) else None
    67→    strict_val = (
    68→        dim_data.get("strict", score_val) if isinstance(dim_data, dict) else None
    69→    )
    70→    if score_val is not None:
    71→        print(
    72→            colorize(
    73→                f"  {display_name}: {score_val:.1f}% health (strict: {strict_val:.1f}%)",
    74→                "bold",
    75→            )
    76→        )
    77→
    78→
    79→def _render_subjective_dimension(
    80→    state: dict,
    81→    config: dict,
    82→    entity,
    83→    pattern_raw: str,
    84→) -> None:
    85→    """Show score + subjective explanation for a subjective dimension."""
    86→    lowered = pattern_raw.strip().lower().replace(" ", "_") if pattern_raw else ""
    87→    dim_data, display_name = _lookup_dimension_score(state, entity.display_name)
    88→    _print_dimension_score(dim_data, display_name)
    89→    print(
    90→        colorize(
    91→            f"  '{pattern_raw.strip()}' is a subjective dimension "
    92→            "— its score comes from design reviews, not code findings.",
    93→            "yellow",
    94→        )
    95→    )
    96→    # Count open review findings tagged with this dimension
    97→    dim_reviews = [
    98→        f
    99→        for f in (state.get("findings") or {}).values()
   100→        if f.get("detector") == "review"
   101→        and f.get("status") == "open"
   102→        and lowered
   103→        in str(f.get("detail", {}).get("dimension", "")).lower().replace(" ", "_")
   104→    ]
   105→    if dim_reviews:
   106→        print(
   107→            colorize(
   108→                f"  {len(dim_reviews)} open review finding(s). "
   109→                "Run `show review --status open`.",
   110→                "dim",
   111→            )
   112→        )
   113→    show_subjective_followup(
   114→        state,
   115→        target_strict_score_from_config(config, fallback=95.0),
   116→    )
   117→
   118→
   119→def _render_clean_mechanical_dimension(state: dict, entity) -> None:
   120→    """Show score + 'no open findings' for a mechanical dimension with zero findings."""
   121→    dim_data, display_name = _lookup_dimension_score(state, entity.display_name)
   122→    _print_dimension_score(dim_data, display_name)
   123→    det_list = ", ".join(entity.detectors) if entity.detectors else "none"
   124→    print(
   125→        colorize(
   126→            f"  No open findings for {entity.display_name}. Detectors: {det_list}",
   127→            "green",
   128→        )
   129→    )
   130→
   131→
   132→def _load_dimension_findings(
   133→    state: dict,
   134→    entity,
   135→    status_filter: str,
   136→) -> list[dict]:
   137→    """Load findings for all detectors in a mechanical dimension."""
   138→    all_matches: list[dict] = []
   139→    for detector in entity.detectors:
   140→        matches = load_matches(
   141→            state, scope=detector, status_filter=status_filter, chronic=False
   142→        )
   143→        all_matches.extend(matches)
   144→    # Deduplicate by finding ID
   145→    seen: set[str] = set()
   146→    unique: list[dict] = []
   147→    for item in all_matches:
   148→        fid = item.get("id", "")
   149→        if fid not in seen:
   150→            seen.add(fid)
   151→            unique.append(item)
   152→    return unique
   153→
   154→
   155→def _render_no_matches(entity, pattern, status_filter, narrative, state, config):
   156→    """Handle the no-findings case: subjective views show dashboard, others show hint."""
   157→    print(
   158→        colorize(f"No {status_filter} findings matching: {pattern}", "yellow")
   159→    )
   160→    write_query(
   161→        {
   162→            "command": "show",
   163→            "query": pattern,
   164→            "status_filter": status_filter,
   165→            "total": 0,
   166→            "findings": [],
   167→            "narrative": narrative,
   168→        }
   169→    )
   170→    if entity.kind == "special_view":
   171→        show_subjective_followup(
   172→            state,
   173→            target_strict_score_from_config(config, fallback=95.0),
   174→        )
   175→    else:
   176→        hint = _detector_names_hint()
   177→        print(
   178→            colorize(
   179→                f"  Try: show <detector>, show <file>, or show subjective. "
   180→                f"Detectors: {hint}",
   181→                "dim",
   182→            )
   183→        )
   184→
   185→
   186→def _render_subjective_views_guide(entity) -> None:
   187→    """Print 'Related views' hint after subjective/subjective_review output."""
   188→    if entity.kind == "special_view" and entity.pattern.strip().lower() in (
   189→        "subjective",
   190→        "subjective_review",
   191→    ):
   192→        print(colorize("  Related views:", "dim"))
   193→        print(colorize("    `show review --status open`            Per-file design review findings", "dim"))
   194→        print(colorize("    `show subjective_review --status open`  Files needing re-review", "dim"))
   195→
   196→
   197→def cmd_show(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   198→    """Show all findings for a file, directory, detector, or pattern."""
   199→    runtime = command_runtime(args)
   200→    state = runtime.state
   201→    config = runtime.config
   202→
   203→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
   204→        return
   205→
   206→    stale_warning = check_tool_staleness(state)
   207→    if stale_warning:
   208→        print(colorize(f"  {stale_warning}", "yellow"))
   209→    skill_warning = check_skill_version()
   210→    if skill_warning:
   211→        print(colorize(f"  {skill_warning}", "yellow"))
   212→    config_warning = check_config_staleness(config)
   213→    if config_warning:
   214→        print(colorize(f"  {config_warning}", "yellow"))
   215→
   216→    print_synthesis_guardrail_info(state=state)
   217→
   218→    pattern_raw = getattr(args, "pattern", "")
   219→    show_code = getattr(args, "code", False)
   220→    chronic = getattr(args, "chronic", False)
   221→    ok, pattern, status_filter, scope = resolve_show_scope(args)
   222→    if not ok or pattern is None:
   223→        return
   224→
   225→    lang = resolve_lang(args)
   226→    lang_name = lang.name if lang else None
   227→    narrative = compute_narrative(
   228→        state,
   229→        context=NarrativeContext(lang=lang_name, command="show"),
   230→    )
   231→
   232→    # --- Entity resolution: classify what the user asked for ---
   233→    entity = resolve_entity(pattern, state)
   234→
   235→    # Dispatch: concerns special view
   236→    if entity.kind == "special_view" and entity.pattern.strip().lower() == "concerns":
   237→        _show_concerns(state, lang_name)
   238→        return
   239→
   240→    # Dispatch: subjective dimension
   241→    if entity.kind == "dimension" and entity.is_subjective:
   242→        _render_subjective_dimension(state, config, entity, pattern_raw)
   243→        return
   244→
   245→    # Dispatch: mechanical dimension
   246→    if entity.kind == "dimension" and not entity.is_subjective:
   247→        matches = _load_dimension_findings(state, entity, status_filter)
   248→        if not matches:
   249→            _render_clean_mechanical_dimension(state, entity)
   250→            return
   251→        pattern = entity.display_name
   252→    else:
   253→        # All other kinds: existing load_matches path
   254→        matches = load_matches(
   255→            state, scope=scope, status_filter=status_filter, chronic=chronic
   256→        )
   257→
   258→    if not matches:
   259→        _render_no_matches(entity, pattern, status_filter, narrative, state, config)
   260→        return
   261→
   262→    (
   263→        surfaced_matches,
   264→        hidden_by_detector,
   265→        noise_budget,
   266→        global_noise_budget,
   267→        budget_warning,
   268→    ) = resolve_noise(
   269→        config,
   270→        matches,
   271→    )
   272→    hidden_total = sum(hidden_by_detector.values())
   273→
   274→    payload = build_show_payload(
   275→        surfaced_matches,
   276→        pattern,
   277→        status_filter,
   278→        ShowPayloadMeta(
   279→            total_matches=len(matches),
   280→            hidden_by_detector=hidden_by_detector,
   281→            noise_budget=noise_budget,
   282→            global_noise_budget=global_noise_budget,
   283→        ),
   284→    )
   285→    write_query({"command": "show", **payload, "narrative": narrative})
   286→
   287→    output_file = getattr(args, "output", None)
   288→    if output_file:
   289→        if write_show_output_file(output_file, payload, len(surfaced_matches)):
   290→            return
   291→        raise SystemExit(1)
   292→
   293→    top = getattr(args, "top", 20) or 20
   294→    render_findings(
   295→        surfaced_matches,
   296→        pattern=pattern,
   297→        status_filter=status_filter,
   298→        show_code=show_code,
   299→        top=top,
   300→        hidden_by_detector=hidden_by_detector,
   301→        hidden_total=hidden_total,
   302→        noise_budget=noise_budget,
   303→        global_noise_budget=global_noise_budget,
   304→        budget_warning=budget_warning,
   305→    )
   306→    try:
   307→        _plan = load_plan()
   308→        _plan_active = _plan if (_plan.get("queue_order") or _plan.get("clusters")) else None
   309→    except PLAN_LOAD_EXCEPTIONS:
   310→        _plan_active = None
   311→    show_agent_plan(narrative, surfaced_matches, plan=_plan_active)
   312→    show_subjective_followup(
   313→        state,
   314→        target_strict_score_from_config(config, fallback=95.0),
   315→    )
   316→
   317→    _render_subjective_views_guide(entity)
   318→
   319→
   320→__all__ = ["cmd_show"]
   321→
```

    83→def synthesis_phase_banner(plan: PlanModel) -> str:
    84→    """Return a banner string when ``synthesis::pending`` is in the queue."""
    85→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
    86→    if SYNTHESIS_ID not in plan.get("queue_order", []):
    87→        return ""
    88→    meta = plan.get("epic_synthesis_meta", {})
    89→    stages = meta.get("synthesis_stages", {})
    90→    completed = [s for s in ("observe", "reflect", "organize") if s in stages]
    91→    if completed:
    92→        return (
    93→            f"Synthesis in progress ({len(completed)}/4 stages complete). "
    94→            "Run: desloppify plan synthesize"
    95→        )
    96→    return (
    97→        "Synthesis needed — review findings require analysis before fixing. "
    98→        "Run: desloppify plan synthesize"
    99→    )
   100→

1→"""Pure mutation operations on the plan model."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import (
     6→    Cluster,
     7→    ExecutionLogEntry,
     8→    PlanModel,
     9→    SkipEntry,
    10→    empty_plan,
    11→    ensure_plan_defaults,
    12→)
    13→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import utc_now
    14→
    15→
    16→_DEFAULT_MAX_LOG_ENTRIES = 10000
    17→
    18→
    19→def _get_log_cap() -> int:
    20→    """Read execution_log_max_entries from config. Returns 0 for unlimited."""
    21→    try:
    22→        from desloppify.core.config import load_config
    23→
    24→        config = load_config()
    25→        value = config.get("execution_log_max_entries", _DEFAULT_MAX_LOG_ENTRIES)
    26→        return max(0, int(value))
    27→    except (ImportError, OSError, ValueError, TypeError):
    28→        return _DEFAULT_MAX_LOG_ENTRIES
    29→
    30→
    31→def append_log_entry(
    32→    plan: PlanModel,
    33→    action: str,
    34→    *,
    35→    finding_ids: list[str] | None = None,
    36→    cluster_name: str | None = None,
    37→    actor: str = "user",
    38→    note: str | None = None,
    39→    detail: dict | None = None,
    40→) -> None:
    41→    """Append a structured entry to the plan's execution log."""
    42→    log = plan.get("execution_log", [])
    43→    entry: ExecutionLogEntry = {
    44→        "timestamp": utc_now(),
    45→        "action": action,
    46→        "finding_ids": finding_ids or [],
    47→        "cluster_name": cluster_name,
    48→        "actor": actor,
    49→        "note": note,
    50→        "detail": detail or {},
    51→    }
    52→    log.append(entry)
    53→    cap = _get_log_cap()
    54→    if cap > 0 and len(log) > cap:
    55→        plan["execution_log"] = log[-cap:]
    56→
    57→
    58→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    59→# Helpers
    60→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    61→
    62→def _remove_id_from_lists(plan: PlanModel, finding_id: str) -> None:
    63→    """Remove a finding ID from queue_order and skipped."""
    64→    order: list[str] = plan["queue_order"]
    65→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan.get("skipped", {})
    66→    if finding_id in order:
    67→        order.remove(finding_id)
    68→    skipped.pop(finding_id, None)
    69→
    70→
    71→def _resolve_position(
    72→    order: list[str],
    73→    position: str,
    74→    target: str | None = None,
    75→    offset: int | None = None,
    76→    finding_ids: list[str] | None = None,
    77→) -> int:
    78→    """Resolve a position specifier to an insertion index.
    79→
    80→    *finding_ids* are the IDs being moved — used to calculate relative
    81→    positions when they already exist in the list.
    82→    """
    83→    moving = set(finding_ids or [])
    84→
    85→    if position == "top":
    86→        return 0
    87→    if position == "bottom":
    88→        return len(order)
    89→
    90→    if position in {"before", "after"} and target:
    91→        return _resolve_relative_position(order, moving, position=position, target=target)
    92→
    93→    if position in {"up", "down"} and offset is not None:
    94→        return _resolve_offset_position(
    95→            order,
    96→            moving,
    97→            position=position,
    98→            offset=offset,
    99→            finding_ids=finding_ids,
   100→        )
   101→
   102→    return len(order)
   103→
   104→
   105→def _resolve_relative_position(
   106→    order: list[str],
   107→    moving: set[str],
   108→    *,
   109→    position: str,
   110→    target: str,
   111→) -> int:
   112→    for i, item_id in enumerate(order):
   113→        if item_id == target and item_id not in moving:
   114→            return i if position == "before" else i + 1
   115→    return 0 if position == "before" else len(order)
   116→
   117→
   118→def _find_index(items: list[str], target: str) -> int | None:
   119→    for i, item_id in enumerate(items):
   120→        if item_id == target:
   121→            return i
   122→    return None
   123→
   124→
   125→def _resolve_offset_position(
   126→    order: list[str],
   127→    moving: set[str],
   128→    *,
   129→    position: str,
   130→    offset: int,
   131→    finding_ids: list[str] | None,
   132→) -> int:
   133→    if not finding_ids:
   134→        return 0 if position == "up" else len(order)
   135→    first_id = finding_ids[0]
   136→    clean_order = [x for x in order if x not in moving]
   137→    current_idx = _find_index(clean_order, first_id)
   138→    if current_idx is None:
   139→        if position == "up":
   140→            return max(0, len(clean_order) - offset)
   141→        return len(clean_order)
   142→    if position == "up":
   143→        return max(0, current_idx - offset)
   144→    return min(len(clean_order), current_idx + offset)
   145→
   146→
   147→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   148→# Move items
   149→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   150→
   151→def move_items(
   152→    plan: PlanModel,
   153→    finding_ids: list[str],
   154→    position: str,
   155→    target: str | None = None,
   156→    offset: int | None = None,
   157→) -> int:
   158→    """Move finding IDs to a position in queue_order. Returns count moved."""
   159→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   160→    order: list[str] = plan["queue_order"]
   161→
   162→    # Remove from skipped if present
   163→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan.get("skipped", {})
   164→    for fid in finding_ids:
   165→        skipped.pop(fid, None)
   166→
   167→    # Remove from current position in order
   168→    for fid in finding_ids:
   169→        if fid in order:
   170→            order.remove(fid)
   171→
   172→    # Resolve insertion point
   173→    idx = _resolve_position(order, position, target, offset, finding_ids)
   174→
   175→    # Insert in original order
   176→    for i, fid in enumerate(finding_ids):
   177→        order.insert(idx + i, fid)
   178→
   179→    # Track user-promoted IDs only when explicitly moved to the front.
   180→    # Moving to bottom/down/after should not create a promotion barrier.
   181→    if position == "top":
   182→        promoted: list[str] = plan.get("promoted_ids", [])
   183→        existing_promoted = set(promoted)
   184→        for fid in finding_ids:
   185→            if fid not in existing_promoted:
   186→                promoted.append(fid)
   187→                existing_promoted.add(fid)
   188→        plan["promoted_ids"] = promoted
   189→
   190→    return len(finding_ids)
   191→
   192→
   193→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   194→# Skip / unskip
   195→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   196→
   197→def skip_items(
   198→    plan: PlanModel,
   199→    finding_ids: list[str],
   200→    *,
   201→    kind: str = "temporary",
   202→    reason: str | None = None,
   203→    note: str | None = None,
   204→    attestation: str | None = None,
   205→    review_after: int | None = None,
   206→    scan_count: int = 0,
   207→) -> int:
   208→    """Move finding IDs to the skipped dict. Returns count skipped."""
   209→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   210→    now = utc_now()
   211→    count = 0
   212→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan["skipped"]
   213→    skip_set = set(finding_ids)
   214→    promoted: list[str] = plan.get("promoted_ids", [])
   215→    plan["promoted_ids"] = [p for p in promoted if p not in skip_set]
   216→    for fid in finding_ids:
   217→        _remove_id_from_lists(plan, fid)
   218→        skipped[fid] = {
   219→            "finding_id": fid,
   220→            "kind": kind,
   221→            "reason": reason,
   222→            "note": note,
   223→            "attestation": attestation,
   224→            "created_at": now,
   225→            "review_after": review_after,
   226→            "skipped_at_scan": scan_count,
   227→        }
   228→        count += 1
   229→    return count
   230→
   231→
   232→def unskip_items(
   233→    plan: PlanModel, finding_ids: list[str]
   234→) -> tuple[int, list[str]]:
   235→    """Bring finding IDs back from skipped to the end of queue_order.
   236→
   237→    Returns ``(count_unskipped, permanent_ids_needing_state_reopen)``
   238→    where the second list contains IDs that were permanent or false_positive
   239→    and need their state-layer status reopened by the caller.
   240→    """
   241→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   242→    count = 0
   243→    need_reopen: list[str] = []
   244→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan["skipped"]
   245→    for fid in finding_ids:
   246→        entry = skipped.pop(fid, None)
   247→        if entry is not None:
   248→            if entry.get("kind") in ("permanent", "false_positive"):
   249→                need_reopen.append(fid)
   250→            if fid not in plan["queue_order"]:
   251→                plan["queue_order"].append(fid)
   252→            count += 1
   253→    return count, need_reopen
   254→
   255→
   256→def resurface_stale_skips(
   257→    plan: PlanModel, current_scan_count: int
   258→) -> list[str]:
   259→    """Move temporary skips past their review_after threshold back to queue.
   260→
   261→    Returns list of resurfaced finding IDs.
   262→    """
   263→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   264→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan["skipped"]
   265→    resurfaced: list[str] = []
   266→    for fid in list(skipped):
   267→        entry = skipped[fid]
   268→        if entry.get("kind") != "temporary":
   269→            continue
   270→        review_after = entry.get("review_after")
   271→        if review_after is None:
   272→            continue
   273→        skipped_at = entry.get("skipped_at_scan", 0)
   274→        if current_scan_count >= skipped_at + review_after:
   275→            skipped.pop(fid)
   276→            if fid not in plan["queue_order"]:
   277→                plan["queue_order"].append(fid)
   278→            resurfaced.append(fid)
   279→    return resurfaced
   280→
   281→
   282→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   283→# Describe / annotate
   284→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   285→
   286→def describe_finding(
   287→    plan: PlanModel, finding_id: str, description: str | None
   288→) -> None:
   289→    """Set or clear an augmented description on a finding."""
   290→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   291→    now = utc_now()
   292→    overrides = plan["overrides"]
   293→    if finding_id not in overrides:
   294→        overrides[finding_id] = {"finding_id": finding_id, "created_at": now}
   295→    overrides[finding_id]["description"] = description
   296→    overrides[finding_id]["updated_at"] = now
   297→
   298→
   299→def annotate_finding(
   300→    plan: PlanModel, finding_id: str, note: str | None
```

   204→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   205→# Stage handlers

   208→def _cmd_stage_observe(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   209→    """Record the OBSERVE stage: agent analyses themes and root causes.
   210→
   211→    No citation gate — the point is genuine analysis, not ID-stuffing.
   212→    Just requires a 100-char report describing what the agent observed.
   213→    """
   214→    report: str | None = getattr(args, "report", None)
   215→    if not report:
   216→        print(colorize("  --report is required for --stage observe.", "red"))
   217→        print(colorize("  Write an analysis of the findings: themes, root causes, contradictions.", "dim"))
   218→        print(colorize("  Identify findings that contradict each other (opposite recommendations).", "dim"))
   219→        print(colorize("  Do NOT just list finding IDs — describe what you actually observe.", "dim"))
   220→        return
   221→
   222→    runtime = command_runtime(args)
   223→    state = runtime.state
   224→    plan = load_plan()
   225→
   226→    if SYNTHESIS_ID not in plan.get("queue_order", []):
   227→        print(colorize("  synthesis::pending is not in the queue \u2014 nothing to observe.", "yellow"))
   228→        return
   229→
   230→    si = collect_synthesis_input(plan, state)
   231→    finding_count = len(si.open_findings)
   232→
   233→    # Edge case: 0 findings
   234→    if finding_count == 0:
   235→        meta = plan.setdefault("epic_synthesis_meta", {})
   236→        stages = meta.setdefault("synthesis_stages", {})
   237→        stages["observe"] = {
   238→            "stage": "observe",
   239→            "report": report,
   240→            "cited_ids": [],
   241→            "timestamp": utc_now(),
   242→            "finding_count": 0,
   243→        }
   244→        save_plan(plan)
   245→        print(colorize("  Observe stage recorded (no findings to analyse).", "green"))
   246→        return
   247→
   248→    # Validation: report length (no citation counting)
   249→    min_chars = 50 if finding_count <= 3 else 100
   250→    if len(report) < min_chars:
   251→        print(colorize(f"  Report too short: {len(report)} chars (minimum {min_chars}).", "red"))
   252→        print(colorize("  Describe themes, root causes, contradictions, and how findings relate.", "dim"))
   253→        return
   254→
   255→    # Save stage (still extract citations for analytics, but don't gate on them)
   256→    valid_ids = set(si.open_findings.keys())
   257→    cited = extract_finding_citations(report, valid_ids)
   258→
   259→    meta = plan.setdefault("epic_synthesis_meta", {})
   260→    stages = meta.setdefault("synthesis_stages", {})
   261→    stages["observe"] = {
   262→        "stage": "observe",
   263→        "report": report,
   264→        "cited_ids": sorted(cited),
   265→        "timestamp": utc_now(),
   266→        "finding_count": finding_count,
   267→    }
   268→    save_plan(plan)
   269→
   270→    append_log_entry(plan, "synthesis_observe", actor="user",
   271→                     detail={"finding_count": finding_count, "cited_ids": sorted(cited)})
   272→    save_plan(plan)
   273→
   274→    print(colorize(
   275→        f"  Observe stage recorded: {finding_count} findings analysed.",
   276→        "green",
   277→    ))
   278→    print(colorize("  Now confirm your analysis.", "yellow"))
   279→    print(colorize("    desloppify plan synthesize --confirm observe", "dim"))
   280→
   281→
   282→def _cmd_stage_reflect(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   283→    """Record the REFLECT stage: compare current findings against completed work.
   284→
   285→    Forces the agent to consider what was previously resolved and whether
   286→    similar issues are recurring. Requires a 100-char report (50 if ≤3 findings).
   287→    If recurring patterns are detected, the report must mention at least one
   288→    recurring dimension name.
   289→    """
   290→    report: str | None = getattr(args, "report", None)
   291→    if not report:
   292→        print(colorize("  --report is required for --stage reflect.", "red"))
   293→        print(colorize("  Compare current findings against completed work and form a holistic strategy:", "dim"))
   294→        print(colorize("  - What clusters were previously completed? Did fixes hold?", "dim"))
   295→        print(colorize("  - Are any dimensions recurring (resolved before, open again)?", "dim"))
   296→        print(colorize("  - What contradictions did you find? Which direction will you take?", "dim"))
   297→        print(colorize("  - Big picture: what to prioritize, what to defer, what to skip?", "dim"))
   298→        return
   299→
   300→    runtime = command_runtime(args)
   301→    state = runtime.state
   302→    plan = load_plan()
   303→
   304→    if SYNTHESIS_ID not in plan.get("queue_order", []):
   305→        print(colorize("  synthesis::pending is not in the queue — nothing to reflect on.", "yellow"))
   306→        return
   307→
   308→    meta = plan.get("epic_synthesis_meta", {})
   309→    stages = meta.get("synthesis_stages", {})
   310→    if "observe" not in stages:
   311→        print(colorize("  Cannot reflect: observe stage not complete.", "red"))
   312→        print(colorize('  Run: desloppify plan synthesize --stage observe --report "..."', "dim"))
   313→        return
   314→    if not stages["observe"].get("confirmed_at"):
   315→        print(colorize("  Cannot reflect: observe stage not confirmed.", "red"))
   316→        print(colorize("  Run: desloppify plan synthesize --confirm observe", "dim"))
   317→        return
   318→
   319→    si = collect_synthesis_input(plan, state)
   320→    finding_count = len(si.open_findings)
   321→
   322→    # Validation: report length
   323→    min_chars = 50 if finding_count <= 3 else 100
   324→    if len(report) < min_chars:
   325→        print(colorize(f"  Report too short: {len(report)} chars (minimum {min_chars}).", "red"))
   326→        print(colorize("  Describe how current findings relate to previously completed work.", "dim"))
   327→        return
   328→
   329→    # Detect recurring patterns
   330→    recurring = detect_recurring_patterns(si.open_findings, si.resolved_findings)
   331→    recurring_dims = sorted(recurring.keys())
   332→
   333→    # If recurring patterns exist, report must mention at least one dimension
   334→    if recurring_dims:
   335→        report_lower = report.lower()
   336→        mentioned = [dim for dim in recurring_dims if dim.lower() in report_lower]
   337→        if not mentioned:
   338→            print(colorize("  Recurring patterns detected but not addressed in report:", "red"))
   339→            for dim in recurring_dims:
   340→                info = recurring[dim]
   341→                print(colorize(
   342→                    f"    {dim}: {len(info['resolved'])} resolved, "
   343→                    f"{len(info['open'])} still open — potential loop",
   344→                    "yellow",
   345→                ))
   346→            print(colorize(
   347→                "  Your report must mention at least one recurring dimension name.",
   348→                "dim",
   349→            ))

  1106→def _validate_attestation(
  1107→    attestation: str,
